Measurement of J / ψ J/\psi –jet correlations in p ​ p pp and Pb + Pb collisions at s NN = 5.02 \sqrt{s_{\text{NN}}}=5.02 TeV with the ATLAS detector


Abstract
Yields of charmonia, bound states of c ​ c ¯ c\bar{c} quarks, are observed to be strongly suppressed in heavy-ion collisions relative to proton–proton collisions as a result of their interaction with the quark-gluon plasma produced in such collisions. Understanding the mechanisms responsible for this suppression requires a detailed understanding of charmonium production. To constrain the production mechanisms, it is essential to quantify how charmonium production correlates with jet activity. Measurements of J / ψ J/\psi production in the dimuon decay channel are presented for inclusive J / ψ J/\psi mesons and for J / ψ J/\psi mesons that are either isolated or not isolated from jets, separately for prompt and non-prompt production. Non-isolated J / ψ J/\psi mesons are defined by matching J / ψ J/\psi candidates to anti- k t k_{t} jets with R = 0.2 R=0.2 and p T > 20 p_{\mathrm{T}}>20 GeV, reconstructed without signal from the J / ψ J/\psi meson, allowing the quantification of jet activity accompanying J / ψ J/\psi production. The analysis uses Pb+Pb and p ​ p pp collisions at s NN = 5.02 \sqrt{s_{\text{NN}}}=5.02 TeV recorded by the ATLAS experiment at the LHC, corresponding to integrated luminosities of 1.82 1.82 nb -1 and 0.26 0.26 fb -1 , respectively. The J / ψ J/\psi yields in Pb+Pb, cross sections in p ​ p pp , and nuclear modification factors are presented for non-isolated, isolated, and inclusive J / ψ J/\psi up to p T = 60 p_{\mathrm{T}}=60 GeV. Isolated fractions and non-prompt fractions are reported in both p ​ p pp and Pb+Pb collisions. Strong J / ψ J/\psi suppression is observed to persist up to the highest measured p T p_{\mathrm{T}} values, with differences observed between non-isolated, isolated, and inclusive J / ψ J/\psi . These results provide new constraints on charmonium production mechanisms and their in-medium suppression.

HION-2018-22

CERN-EP-2026-167

\AtlasJournal

1
Introduction

High-energy heavy-ion collisions at the Relativistic Heavy Ion Collider (RHIC)
[
1
]
and the Large Hadron Collider (LHC)
[
2
]
create conditions under that strongly interacting matter transitions into the deconfined phase of quantum chromodynamics (QCD), the quark-gluon plasma (QGP)
[
3
,
4
]
. In this environment, hard probes –
particles or partonic systems produced in large-momentum-transfer scatterings that precede the formation of the medium –
serve as sensitive tools for characterising the medium. Energetic partons undergo substantial energy loss, giving rise to the well-established phenomenon of jet quenching
[
5
,
6
,
7
]
, while heavy quark–antiquark bound states (quarkonia) are modified through colour screening, in-medium dissociation, and regeneration
[
8
]
. The resulting suppression of quarkonium production is found to be pronounced and to persist up to transverse momenta of several tens of
GeV

[
9
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
,
16
]
. In the case of
c\bar{c}
bound states (charmonia), besides the conventional suppression mechanisms associated with colour screening and in-medium dissociation, it has also been suggested that charmonia with high transverse momenta (
p_{\text{T}}
) may be affected by medium-induced gluon radiation, leading to an energy-loss mechanism analogous to that responsible for jet quenching
[
17
,
18
,
19
]
.

Understanding the origin of charmonia suppression requires a clear picture of how charmonia are produced in high-energy collisions. Charmonium states may be created directly in the hard scattering and subsequently evolve as either colour-singlet or colour-octet configurations
[
20
,
21
,
22
,
8
]
, or they may be formed during a parton-shower evolution
[
23
,
24
]
. Although extensive theoretical work is devoted to charmonia production, the dominant production mechanism and the possible interplay among different production mechanisms remain unresolved
[
25
]
. Additional complexity arises from the distinction between prompt charmonia, formed on the time scale of the strong interaction, and non-prompt charmonia, which appear much later through
b
-hadron decays. To constrain the production mechanisms, it is therefore important to experimentally quantify how charmonium production correlates with jet activity.

To quantify the correlation between the charmonium production and jet activity, the CMS and LHCb collaborations measured the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
–jet fragmentation function in 5.02
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions
[
26
]
and 13
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions
[
27
]
, respectively. In those measurements, the fragmentation function corresponds to the distribution of
z
, with
z
defined as the ratio of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
to the
p_{\text{T}}
of the jet into which the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is clustered. The prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons were found to have more surrounding jet activity, i.e., to populate lower values of
z
than predicted by
Pythia
8
[
28
]
simulations, suggesting that
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production late in the parton shower is generally underestimated in the model
[
24
]
. The correlation between the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production and jet activity was also studied at the level of yields in 8
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions by CMS
[
29
]
. That measurement used events where at least one jet with
p_{\text{T}}>25

GeV
was observed and evaluated the angular separation between the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
meson and the jet, finding that most prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons with energy above 15
GeV
and rapidity
|y|<1
are contained in jets.

While the existing
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
–jet correlation measurements provide important constraints to the theoretical models describing charmonia production, they still do not give complete information. In particular, the definition of whether the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is contained in a jet depends on the jet definition. As the jet four-momentum is constructed from a sum containing the four-momentum of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, an otherwise isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with high enough
p_{\text{T}}
is by definition a jet, thus in a jet, whereas the isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with
p_{\text{T}}
lower than the minimum value used to define a jet is not in a jet
[
25
]
. In addition, studying the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in an event sample that is triggered by the presence of a jet can introduce a possible event selection bias.

The first goal of the measurement presented in this paper is to bring new information into the quantification of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
–jet correlation by separating the kinematics of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
from the kinematics of jets and by analysing the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
–jet correlations in all
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events rather than in events a priori containing jets. The second goal is to measure possible differences between the suppression of isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
produced in association with jets in Pb+Pb collisions.

The measurement presented in this paper uses data collected by the ATLAS experiment in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions in 2015 and 2018, and in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions collected in 2017, both at a centre-of-mass energy of 5.02
TeV
per colliding nucleon pair.
The Pb+Pb and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data samples contain
1.82
nb
-1
and
0.26
fb
-1
of collision data, respectively. The measurement thus presents the quantification of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
suppression using the full LHC Run 2 data sample of
Pb
+
Pb
collisions.

2
ATLAS detector

The ATLAS detector
[
30
]
at the LHC covers nearly the entire solid angle around the collision point.
1
1

1

ATLAS uses a right-handed coordinate system with its origin at the nominal interaction point (IP)
in the centre of the detector and the
z
-axis along the beam pipe.
The
x
-axis points from the IP to the centre of the LHC ring,
and the
y
-axis points upwards.
Polar coordinates
(r,\phi)
are used in the transverse plane,

\phi
being the azimuthal angle around the
z
-axis.
The pseudorapidity is defined in terms of the polar angle
\theta
as
\eta=-\ln\tan(\theta/2)
and is equal to the rapidity

y=\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{E+p_{z}}{E-p_{z}}\right)
in the relativistic limit.
Angular distance is measured in units of
\Delta R\equiv\sqrt{(\Delta y)^{2}+(\Delta\phi)^{2}}
.

It consists of an inner tracking detector surrounded by a thin superconducting solenoid, electromagnetic and hadronic calorimeters,
and a muon spectrometer incorporating three large superconducting air-core toroidal magnets.

The inner-detector system (ID) is immersed in a
2\text{\,}\mathrm{T}
axial magnetic field
and provides charged-particle tracking in the range of
|\eta|<2.5
.
The high-granularity silicon pixel detector covers the vertex region and typically provides four measurements per track,
the first hit generally being in the insertable B-layer (IBL) installed before Run 2
[
31
,
32
]
.
It is followed by the SemiConductor Tracker (SCT), which usually provides eight measurements per track.
These silicon detectors are complemented by the transition radiation tracker (TRT),
which enables radially extended track reconstruction up to
|\eta|=2.0
.
The TRT also provides electron identification information
based on the fraction of hits (typically 30 in total) above a higher energy-deposit threshold corresponding to transition radiation.

The calorimeter system covers the pseudorapidity range
|\eta|<4.9
.
Within the region
|\eta|<3.2
, electromagnetic calorimetry is provided by barrel and
endcap high-granularity lead/liquid-argon (LAr) calorimeters,
with an additional thin LAr presampler covering
|\eta|<1.8

to correct for energy loss in material upstream of the calorimeters.
Hadronic calorimetry is provided by the steel/scintillator-tile calorimeter,
segmented into three barrel structures within
|\eta|<1.7
, and two copper/LAr hadronic endcap calorimeters.
The solid angle coverage is completed with copper/LAr and tungsten/LAr calorimeter modules (FCal),
covering the forward regions of
3.1<|\eta|<4.9
.
The zero-degree calorimeters (ZDC)
[
33
]
measure neutral particles at pseudorapidities
|\eta|\geq 8.3
and consist of layers of alternating quartz rods and tungsten plates. ZDC modules are positioned symmetrically at
z=\pm 140\penalty\ \mathrm{m}
.

The muon spectrometer (MS) comprises separate trigger and
high-precision tracking chambers measuring the deflection of muons in a magnetic field generated by the superconducting air-core toroidal magnets.
The field integral of the toroids ranges between
2.0
and
6.0\text{\,}\mathrm{T}\text{\,}\mathrm{m}

across most of the detector.
Three layers of precision chambers, each consisting of layers of monitored drift tubes (MDTs), cover the region
|\eta|<2.7
,
complemented by cathode-strip chambers (CSCs) in the forward region, where the background is highest.
The muon trigger system covers the range
|\eta|<2.4
with resistive-plate chambers (RPCs) in the barrel, and thin-gap chambers (TGCs) in the endcap regions.

The luminosity is measured mainly by the LUCID–2
[
34
]
detector that records Cherenkov light produced in the quartz windows of photomultipliers located close to the beampipe.

A two-level trigger system is used to select events of interest. The first-level (L1) trigger system is implemented in custom hardware, followed by selections made by software algorithms in the high-level trigger (HLT)
[
35
]
. The L1 trigger accepts events from the
40\text{\,}\mathrm{MHz}
bunch crossings at a rate below
100\text{\,}\mathrm{kHz}
, which the high-level trigger further reduces to record complete events to disk at about
1\text{\,}\mathrm{kHz}
. The L1 muon trigger requires coincidences between hits on different RPC or TGC planes, which are used as a seed for the HLT algorithms. The HLT uses dedicated algorithms to incorporate information from both the MS and the ID, achieving position and momentum resolution close to that provided by the offline muon reconstruction
[
36
]
.

A software suite
[
37
]
is used in data simulation, in the reconstruction
and analysis of real and simulated data, in detector operations, and in the trigger and data acquisition
systems of the experiment.

3
Data and simulated event samples

Events in the
Pb
+
Pb
and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions are collected using a trigger requiring the presence of at least two muons, both with
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}>4

GeV
. In the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
sample, both muons must generate a spatially separated L1 muon trigger region of interest (RoI) and be confirmed by the HLT, while in the
Pb
+
Pb
sample, only one muon is required to be seen by the L1 muon trigger and confirmed by the HLT, the second muon is only required to satisfy the HLT requirements
[
36
]
.
In addition to the muon trigger, two triggers are used in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions to select minimum-bias events. These are based on the presence of at least
50\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
of transverse energy
in all sections of the calorimeter system (
|\eta|<4.9
) or, for events that do not meet this condition, on the presence of energy deposition above a one neutron threshold in both ZDC arms.

Both
Pb
+
Pb
and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data also contain signal from multiple inelastic interactions per bunch crossing, also referred to as pile-up. The average number of inelastic interactions per bunch crossing reaches a maximum value of 0.004 in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions, and it varies between 1.4 and 4.4 in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions. Although only a small fraction of the
Pb
+
Pb
events (
<0.5
%) contain pile-up events, these were suppressed utilising the observed anti-correlation, expected from the nuclear geometry, between the total transverse energy deposited in both of the forward calorimeters,
\Sigma E_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{FCal}}
, and the energy in the ZDC. Pile-up collisions are not rejected in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions.

Monte Carlo (MC) simulated event samples are used for performance studies and to correct for detector effects. Prompt
(pp\rightarrow J/\psi\rightarrow\mu\mu)
and non-prompt
(pp\rightarrow b\bar{b}\rightarrow J/\psi\rightarrow\mu\mu)
samples of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
were produced using
Pythia
8.2
[
38
]
for event generation and
Photos

[
39
]
for electromagnetic radiation corrections. The A14 set of tuned parameters
[
40
]
is used together with CTEQ6L1 parton distribution function set
[
41
]
. The response of the ATLAS detector is simulated using
Geant4

[
42
,
43
]
. To simulate the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production in the high multiplicity environment of
Pb
+
Pb
collisions, the simulated
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
events are overlaid with dedicated minimum-bias
Pb
+
Pb
data events
[
44
]
. The minimum-bias data events from
Pb
+
Pb
collisions are combined with the signal from the
Pythia
8 simulation of hard scattering events at the digitisation stage, and then reconstructed as a combined event.

In
Pb
+
Pb
collision data and MC samples, the transverse energy at the electromagnetic scale deposited in the forward calorimeter,
\Sigma E_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{FCal}}
, is used to estimate the centrality of the collision. Centrality describes the degree of geometric overlap of two colliding nuclei in the plane perpendicular to the beam, with large overlap in central collisions and small overlap in peripheral collisions.
The measurement is performed in four exclusive centrality intervals, 0–10%, 10–20%, 20–40%, 40–80%, and an inclusive 0–80% centrality interval. The centrality is defined such that smaller percentiles label more central collisions.
A weight is assigned to each MC event, ensuring that the event sample obtained from the minimum-bias trigger entering the MC overlay has the same centrality distribution as that measured in the data. The
TGlauberMC
v3.2 package
[
45
]
, providing improved implementation of the MC Glauber model
[
46
]
, is used to calculate the mean nuclear overlap function,
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle

[
46
,
47
]
, as well as its uncertainty, for each centrality interval as presented in Table
1
. The quantity
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
is proportional to the mean number of binary nucleon–nucleon collisions divided by the inelastic nucleon–nucleon cross section, and accounts for the nuclear overlap geometry in a given centrality interval.

The number of minimum-bias events,
N_{\mathrm{evt}}
, is used to normalise the yield in the respective centrality interval. Minimum-bias events are selected by requiring that they pass at least one of the two minimum-bias triggers. The analysed data sample corresponds to
11.15\times 10^{9}

Pb
+
Pb
collisions after correction for the trigger prescale factor.

Table 1:

The
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
values and corresponding uncertainties for each centrality interval. The centrality-inclusive
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
corresponds to the weighted sum of
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
in individual centrality intervals.

Centrality

\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
[
\mathrm{mb}^{-1}
]

\sigma(\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle)
[
\mathrm{mb}^{-1}
]

4
Data analysis

The
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons are reconstructed in the dimuon (
\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
) decay channel.
The reconstructed muons are required to satisfy the
Medium
muon working point, ignoring the TRT requirements
[
48
]
, have
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}>4

GeV
,
|\eta^{\mu}|

<2.4
, and be matched to the muons reconstructed at the trigger level. Pairs of muons with opposite charges are used to construct dimuon objects. The transverse momentum of the dimuon,
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}
, is required to be larger than 9
GeV
, and the rapidity of the dimuon,
y_{\mu\mu}
, is required to have an absolute value of less than 2. In addition, the invariant mass of the dimuon,
m_{\mu\mu}
, has to be within an interval of 2.5
GeV
and 3.5
GeV
. The selected dimuon is refitted to a common vertex.
The yields entering the evaluation of observables are extracted using two-dimensional unbinned maximum-likelihood fits
[
49
]
in dimuon invariant mass
m_{\mu\mu}
and pseudo-proper lifetime
\tau_{\mu\mu}
defined as
[
11
]

\tau_{\mu\mu}=\frac{L_{xy}m_{\mu\mu}}{p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mu\mu}},

(1)

where
L_{xy}
is the distance between the reconstructed primary vertex and the reconstructed dimuon vertex in the event on the transverse plane. The
\tau_{\mu\mu}
is introduced to separate prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in the fitting procedure.

The raw dimuon kinematic distributions entering the fitting procedure are corrected for detector-related effects, as described in Section
4.1
. The dimuon candidates are tagged for the presence of jets, which is described in Section
4.2
. The fit model and the extraction of corrected yields are described in Section
4.3
. Final observables extracted using corrected yields are defined in Section
4.4
.

4.1
Corrections of raw
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
distributions

Before the dimuon
m_{\mu\mu}
and
\tau_{\mu\mu}
distributions are fit to extract
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yields, each candidate dimuon is corrected using a weight that accounts for the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
dimuon acceptance, trigger efficiency, and reconstruction efficiency.
The dimuon acceptance
\mathcal{A}(\psi(\text{nS}))
(
|y^{\mu\mu}|
,
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}
) is defined as the probability that the
\psi
(nS) decay products fall within the fiducial volume (
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}

>

4\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
,
|\eta^{\mu}|

<

2.4\text{\,}
). The acceptance is obtained from a generator-level

?
accept-reject
?
simulation as described in Ref.
[
50
]
.
Finite detector and reconstruction resolution might lead to the application of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
acceptance correction to the
{\mathit{{\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}{\mathit{{\left({2S}\right)}}}
candidates and vice versa, therefore the upper mass boundary for the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates of 3.5
GeV
is used and the lower mass boundary for the
{\mathit{{\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}{\mathit{{\left({2S}\right)}}}
candidates is set to be 3.2
GeV
, resulting in the overlap range of 0.3
GeV
. The interpolation of the acceptances is then performed as

\mathcal{A}(|y^{\mu\mu}|,\,p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu})=\begin{cases}\mathcal{A}(J/\psi)&\text{if }m_{\mu\mu}\leq 3.2\ \text{$\text{GeV}$ }\\
\mathcal{A}(J/\psi)\cdot\frac{3.5-m_{\mu\mu}\ [\text{$\text{GeV}$ }]}{0.3}+\mathcal{A}(\psi(2S))\cdot\frac{m_{\mu\mu}\ [\text{$\text{GeV}$ }]-3.2}{0.3}&\text{if }3.2<m_{\mu\mu}<3.5\ \text{$\text{GeV}$ }.\\
\end{cases}

(2)

To account for the final state radiation (FSR) of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
decay products, an additional correction is derived using the comparison of the acceptance from the accept-reject simulation and the acceptance obtained from the
Pythia
8 samples. In principle, the acceptance could also depend on the spin alignment of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. In this analysis,
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons are assumed to be produced unpolarised, following Refs.
[
51
,
52
,
53
]
. The effects of variations to this assumption are considered and discussed in Section
5
.

The dimuon reconstruction efficiency,
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reco}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
, is determined as the product of two single-muon reconstruction efficiencies. Muon reconstruction in ATLAS is performed independently in the ID and MS. The total single-muon reconstruction efficiency can be factorised into the ID track reconstruction efficiency and MS reconstruction efficiency. The single muon reconstruction efficiency for the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data is obtained from the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}

Pythia
8 simulation. The additional data-to-MC scale factors are derived using
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}
tag and probe method
[
54
]
using 13
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data, as the previous study showed no dependence of the MS efficiency on the pile-up conditions
[
55
]
. The combined ID+MS muon reconstruction efficiency for the
Pb
+
Pb
data is obtained using the tag and probe method in
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}

Pythia
8 events overlaid with dedicated minimum-bias
Pb
+
Pb
data events as described in Section
3
. Data-to-MC scale factors are determined in
Pb
+
Pb
data to account for the small differences between data and simulation. The scale factors are close to unity in the barrel region and reach values of 1.05 at low
p_{\text{T}}
in the endcap regions. The MS+ID efficiency at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=4

GeV
is about 55% in the barrel region (
|\eta^{\mu}|

<1.05
) and 70% in the endcap region (
1.05<|\eta^{\mu}|<2.4
). The efficiency saturates around 95% at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=9

GeV
for both barrel and endcap regions. The performance of the muon reconstruction at low
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}
in the barrel region is affected mainly by the muon energy loss in the calorimeter system and the presence of multiple scattering.

The dimuon trigger efficiency,
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{trig}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
, is constructed from the single-muon trigger efficiencies in the input. The single-muon trigger employs two distinct logic schemes: a) a L1-seeded muon trigger, which requires the formation of a L1 RoI of the muon candidate that is subsequently confirmed in the HLT trigger sequence, and b) a HLT-only muon trigger, which is only performed at the HLT level without any L1 trigger requirement.
The single-muon trigger efficiency is determined in
Pythia
MC simulation. Data-to-MC scale factors are determined as the ratio of the trigger efficiency evaluated in the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data to that evaluated in the MC, to correct for differences between the data and the simulation. Both scale factors and efficiencies are evaluated using a
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}
tag-and-probe method
[
54
]
.
The dimuon trigger efficiency in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions is factorised as the product of two L1-seeded muon trigger efficiencies. For the L1-seeded single-muon trigger efficiency, an additional centrality-dependent correction is determined as the ratio of the single-muon trigger efficiency measured in the
Pb
+
Pb
data as a function of centrality to the single-muon trigger efficiency measured in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data. The L1-seeded single-muon trigger efficiency varies from 60% at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=4

GeV
to 70% at higher
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}
(
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=10

GeV
) in the barrel region, and it varies from 50% at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=4

GeV
to 90% at higher
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}
(
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=10

GeV
) in the endcaps. The inefficiency originates primarily from the L1 trigger, where the RoI formation requires hit coincidences across multiple RPC and TGC stations. In 0–10% central collisions, the centrality-dependent correction corresponds to 0.7 at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=4

GeV
, and it reaches unity at
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=10

GeV
and
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu}=15

GeV
in the barrel and endcap, respectively. In 40–80%, the centrality-dependent correction is unity in both barrel and endcap regions. The centrality dependence arises from reduced tracking resolution in the trigger turn-on region, especially in central collisions, where the tracking resolution worsens due to an increase in random hit density from other tracks. The HLT-only single-muon trigger is only used in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions, and its efficiency is again determined using the tag and probe method. The HLT-only single-muon trigger efficiency plateau is found to be 90% in both the barrel and endcap regions, regardless of energy threshold. The dimuon trigger efficiency in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions is factorised into a form consisting of both L1-seeded and HLT-only muon trigger efficiencies, as previously used in Refs.
[
11
,
14
,
56
]
.

The total weight applied to raw dimuon distributions is then

w=\frac{1}{\mathcal{A}\times\varepsilon_{\mathrm{trig}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\times\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reco}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}.

(3)

The denominator of this weight is multiplied by jet-related corrections defined in the next section.

4.2
Dimuon–jet association

Jets entering this analysis are reconstructed using the anti-
k_{t}
algorithm
[
57
,
58
]
with a radius parameter of
R=0.2
, which provides superior performance over the
R=0.4
jets in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions in terms of the jet energy resolution (JER) and jet reconstruction efficiency. The jet reconstruction procedure follows that used previously in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions
[
59
,
60
]
, and is summarised here. Calorimeter cells in all calorimeter layers, with their energy at the electromagnetic scale, are grouped into
\Delta\eta\times\Delta\phi=0.1\times\pi/32
calorimetric towers. The towers are used as the input to the initial jet-finding. After the initial jet-finding, the contribution from the underlying event (UE) to the energy deposited in the towers is estimated on an event-by-event basis.
This is done by averaging over energies deposited in towers at a given
\eta
position. During the averaging and subsequent subtraction, the
\phi
dependence arising from the collective flow in Pb+Pb collisions is also taken into account.
Information from towers within
\Delta R=0.4
of jet candidates is excluded from the estimate of UE energy. The energies in towers inside jets are updated to subtract the estimated UE contribution, and the UE procedure is iterated using a better-defined set of jets to define the exclusion regions. The resulting jet kinematics are corrected using
p_{\text{T}}
- and
\eta
-dependent factors, determined from simulation, to account for the response of the calorimeter to jets
[
61
]
.
An additional correction for the absolute response in data is based on
in situ
studies of
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions where jets recoil against photons,
Z
bosons, or jets in other regions of the calorimeter
[
61
]
. This calibration is followed by a cross-calibration that relates the jet energy scale (JES) in high-luminosity 13
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions
[
62
]
, used for
in situ
studies, to that of jets reconstructed in the 5.02
TeV
data. The same jet reconstruction procedure, without the azimuthal modulation of the UE, is also applied to
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions. The UE subtraction in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions removes the pile-up contribution to the jet.

?
Truth
?
-level jets are defined in the MC samples before detector simulation by applying the anti-
k_{t}
algorithm with
R=0.2
to stable particles with a proper lifetime greater than 30 ps, but excluding muons and neutrinos, which do not leave significant energy deposits in the calorimeter. After the detector simulation, the truth jets are matched to the nearest reconstructed jet in
\Delta R<0.15
.

Jets are used to tag dimuon candidates before the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal extraction by the fitting procedure described in Section
4.3
. For each dimuon candidate passing the selection criteria, a search for a reconstructed jet with transverse momentum
p_{\text{T}}>$20\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
matching the angular distance
\Delta R(\mathrm{dimuon},\mathrm{jet})<0.4
is performed.
The
\Delta R
requirement is chosen to associate
J/\psi
candidates with nearby jets using a cone size comparable to a jet radius commonly used in LHC jet measurements
[
63
,
64
]
.

Presence of the matching jet then allows the dimuon candidates to be separated into three classes:
non-isolated
,
isolated
, and
inclusive
.
Non-isolated dimuon candidates refer to those with a matched jet, while the inclusive class refers to all dimuon candidates. The yield in the isolated class is calculated using the yield differences between inclusive and non-isolated classes as determined in the fit procedure described in Section
4.3
.

To correct the dimuon tagging for finite jet momentum resolution and jet reconstruction efficiencies, jet
p_{\text{T}}
threshold correction factors are derived using the non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
MC samples defined in Section
3
. The correction factors are obtained as a ratio of the yield of reconstructed dimuons associated with the truth jet to the yield of reconstructed dimuons associated with the reconstructed jet in bins of reconstructed jet
p_{\text{T}}
and reconstructed dimuon
p_{\text{T}}
. These factors are applied as an additional weight
w_{\mathrm{jet}}
on non-isolated dimuon candidates entering the fitting described in Section
4.3
. The reconstructed jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectra entering the correction factors are reweighted to account for the difference in the shape of the reconstructed jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum between data and MC, and the associated systematic uncertainty is evaluated. These correction factors also account for muon energy deposits in the calorimeter.
The procedure is validated by an MC closure test, with the closure consistent with unity.

4.3
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
signal extraction

The yields of prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons are determined using unbinned maximum-likelihood fits to the weighted (as defined in Equation
3
) dimuon
m_{\mu\mu}
and
\tau_{\mu\mu}
distributions, following the same procedure for both
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and Pb+Pb data. The fits are done separately for each
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}
and centrality interval, non-isolated, and inclusive class. The yield of the isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
,
N_{\mathrm{iso}}
, is extracted from the yield of the non-isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
,
N_{\mathrm{non-iso}}
, and inclusive yield,
N_{\mathrm{incl}}
using the difference

N_{\mathrm{iso}}=N_{\mathrm{incl}}-N_{\mathrm{non-iso}}.

(4)

The probability distribution function (PDF) for the fit, as previously validated in Refs.
[
50
,
11
]
, is defined as a normalised sum of five terms corresponding to the one prompt signal source, one non-prompt signal source, one prompt background source and two non-prompt background sources. Each term is factorised into dimuon
m_{\mu\mu}
-dependent and
\tau_{\mu\mu}
-dependent functions. The compact form of the PDF can be written as

\text{PDF}(m_{\mu\mu},\tau_{\mu\mu})=\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{N}_{i}h_{i}(m_{\mu\mu})\cdot t_{i}(\tau_{\mu\mu})\ast g_{i}(\tau_{\mu\mu}),

(5)

where
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is the normalisation factor,
h_{i}(m_{\mu\mu})
is a function describing the dimuon
m_{\mu\mu}
signal or background distribution,
t_{i}(\tau_{\mu\mu})
is a function describing the dimuon
\tau_{\mu\mu}
signal or background distribution, and
g_{i}(\tau_{\mu\mu})
is a function describing the detector resolution defined as a double Gaussian distribution. The symbol

?
\ast
?
denotes the convolution. The distribution functions
h_{i}
and
t_{i}
are defined using the following distribution functions: the Crystal Ball (CB) function
[
65
]
, the Gaussian (G) distribution function, and the exponential (E) distribution function. All individual components are fully defined in Table
2
. The fit is performed using the RooFit framework
[
66
]
. To stabilise the fit model and reduce parameter correlation, a number of component terms listed in Table
2
share common parameters. Some terms are scaled to each other by a multiplicative parameter, while others are fixed to the values observed in the MC simulation.

The signal mass shape is modelled as the sum of the Crystal-Ball distribution and a single Gaussian distribution for the prompt (
i=1
in Table
2
) and non-prompt (
i=2
) signal. These CB and Gaussian distributions share a common mean. The
\alpha
parameter represents the fractional contribution of the CB and Gaussian signal distributions. The width term in the CB distribution corresponds to the width of the Gaussian distribution multiplied by the scaling factor. The tail parameters of the CB distribution, as well as the width-scaling factor, are fixed based on the MC simulations. Variations of these parameters are considered as part of the fit model systematic uncertainties. The background mass shape is divided into prompt (
i=3
) and two non-prompt parts (
i=4
,
i=5
), modelled by three exponentials.
No separate
\psi(2S)
signal component is included in the fit, since the fitted mass range ends well below the
\psi(2S)
mass. Any residual contribution from its low-mass tail is absorbed by the smooth background parameterisation.

The
\tau_{\mu\mu}
distribution is composed of the prompt and non-prompt components. The prompt component is modelled as a delta function (
i=1
in Table
2
) convolved with the double-Gaussian resolution function. The resolution function has a fixed mean at
\tau=0
. The width terms are free but connected by the scaling term between two Gaussian distributions, whose value is fixed. The variation of this parameter is considered part of the fit model systematic uncertainty. The non-prompt pseudo-proper decay time is described as a single-sided exponential distribution (
i=2
) convolved with a double-Gaussian resolution function. The background
\tau_{\mu\mu}
distribution is also composed of the prompt and non-prompt components. The prompt component is described by a delta function convolved with the double-Gaussian resolution function (
i=3
). The non-prompt component is modelled by a single-sided exponential distribution for positive values of
\tau
(
i=4
), and by a double-sided exponential (
i=5
) that accounts for mis-reconstructed dimuons or Drell–Yan muons and combinatorial background. The single-sided and double-sided exponential distributions are convolved with the same double-Gaussian resolution distributions that are used for the signal part.

Table 2:

\alpha
represents the fractional contribution of the Crystal-Ball (CB) and Gaussian (G) signal distributions, the
\delta
(
\tau_{\mu\mu}
) is the Dirac delta function, and the
E
denotes the exponential function.

An example of the fit for the 0–80% centrality interval of
Pb
+
Pb
collisions projected onto the binned representation of data distributions is shown in panels (a,b) in Figure
1
, a corresponding example of the fit for the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data is presented in panels (c,d) in Figure
1
.

((a))

Binned representation of (a,c) the weighted dimuon invariant-mass
m_{\mu\mu}
distribution and (b,d) the dimuon pseudo-proper decay-time
\tau_{\mu\mu}
distribution in the fitted range for candidates in the transverse-momentum interval 9
GeV

<p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}<
10
GeV
and
|y^{\mu\mu}|<2
in (a,b) 0–80% Pb
+

pp
collisions. The fit results are shown as solid lines, while the coloured filled areas indicate the extracted signal and background components. The error bars correspond to the statistical uncertainties of the data points.

The UE in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions, pile-up in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions, and the multi-parton interactions (MPI) in both
Pb
+
Pb
and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions may lead to the presence of jets produced in
\Delta R<0.4
from the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, but not originating from the same hard process as the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. The yield of these background jets is estimated by using the orthogonal cone method
[
67
]
. Using that method, the background jet yield is estimated as a yield of jets in the event with
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in a cone of radius
R=0.4
centred at coordinates
(\phi_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}},-\eta_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}})
in the azimuth-pseudorapidity space. In the region near
\eta=0
where the background cone would overlap with the signal cone, the
\eta
coordinate is shifted by a constant value and the yield is re-weighted by a factor compensating for the difference in the average yields taken from simulation. The estimated background jet yield is applied as an additive correction to transfer the corresponding yield from the non-isolated to the isolated category, as described by Equation
4
.
The yield of background jets is estimated to be less than 1% of the isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yield in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions and less than 10% in 0–10% central
Pb
+
Pb
collisions.
The procedure is tested on jet-tagged dimuon candidates in MC, resulting in full recovery of the jet-tagged signal distributions. The difference in the background yield magnitude between data and MC is less than 2%.

To connect the results of this paper with previous publications
[
26
,
27
,
29
]
and to do a qualitative test of the jet association procedure, the raw fragmentation function of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, i.e. the fragmentation function not corrected for the detector response, is evaluated. In this case, the fraction
z
of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
momentum to the momentum of the jet is estimated as

z=\frac{p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}}{p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{jet}}+p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}},

(6)

where
p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{jet}}
represents the momentum of the jet reconstructed without the signal from the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
meson, and the denominator of Equation
6
thus follows the definition used by the previous measurements
[
26
,
27
,
29
]
.
The raw fragmentation function is then defined as

D(z)=\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{jet}}}\frac{dN(z)}{dz},

(7)

representing the yield (
N
) of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons carrying a fraction of momentum
z
normalised by the number of jets (
N_{\mathrm{jet}}
) containing a
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. The raw fragmentation functions are evaluated for non-isolated prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
candidates selected based on conditions
3.0<m_{\mu\mu}<3.2

GeV
,
-0.3<\tau_{\mu\mu}<0.3
ps and
9<p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}<60

GeV
, and they are not unfolded for the detector response and not corrected for the background. The resulting raw fragmentation functions evaluated in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions are shown in Figure
2
.
The raw fragmentation function evaluated in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions is directly compared to the raw fragmentation functions with the same kinematic cuts evaluated using leading-order (LO) QCD
Pythia
8 samples as defined in Section
3
. The measured raw fragmentation functions for prompt dimuon candidates are qualitatively consistent with the fragmentation function simulated in
Pythia
8 for non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production and differ significantly from those simulated for prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production. This confirms earlier findings by CMS Collaboration published in Ref.
[
26
]
.

Figure 2:
Raw fragmentation function evaluated for non-isolated prompt
J/\psi
candidates in
pp
collisions. The
J/\psi
candidates were selected requiring
2.5<m_{\mu\mu}<3.5

GeV
,
-0.3<\tau_{\mu\mu}<0.3
ps and
9<p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}<60

GeV
. Jets with
p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{jet}}>20

GeV
are used. Results are compared with the LO QCD
Pythia
prediction of raw distributions for prompt and non-prompt dimuon candidates with the same kinematic requirements as those applied to the data. The vertical lines represent statistical uncertainties. The statistical uncertainties of the black markers are negligible and thus not visible.

4.4
Definition of observables

The measurement presented in this paper quantifies
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
cross-section and yields in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
Pb
+
Pb
collisions, respectively. Using cross-section and yields, the nuclear modification factor of the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
meson is evaluated. These observables are evaluated for prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, which also allows the determination of the fraction of non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons. These observables are also evaluated for non-isolated, isolated, and inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons, which allows the determination of the fraction of isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons. The definition of all observables is given in this section.

The differential cross section for
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}
production in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions is defined as

\mathcal{B}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d^{2}}\sigma_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}{\mathrm{d}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\mathrm{d}y_{\mu\mu}}=\frac{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{corr}}}{\mathrm{\Delta}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\times\mathrm{\Delta}y_{\mu\mu}\times L},

(8)

where
\mathcal{B}(\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}})=(5.93\pm 0.06)\%

[
68
]
is the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
dimuon decay branching fraction,
N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{corr}}
is the corrected
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yield estimated by using the procedure discussed in Section
4.3
,
p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}
and
y_{\mu\mu}
are the dimuon transverse momentum and rapidity, respectively, and
L
is the integrated luminosity of the
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data sample.

The yield characterising the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\rightarrow\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{+}}}{}\mathit{{\mu}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{-}}}
production in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions is defined as

\mathcal{B}\cdot\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}{\mathrm{d}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\mathrm{d}y_{\mu\mu}}\right|_{\mathrm{cent}}=\left.\frac{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{corr}}}{\mathrm{\Delta}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\times\mathrm{\Delta}y_{\mu\mu}}\right|_{\mathrm{cent}},

(9)

where

?
cent
?
refers to a specific centrality interval.

In nucleus–nucleus collisions, the suppression of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production is quantified using the nuclear modification factor,
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
, which is defined for each centrality interval as

\left.R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}\right|_{\mathrm{cent}}=\frac{\left.\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{evt}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}{\mathrm{d}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\mathrm{d}y_{\mu\mu}}\right|_{\mathrm{cent}}}{\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle|_{\mathrm{cent}}\times\frac{\mathrm{d^{2}}\sigma_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}{\mathrm{d}p_{\text{T}}^{\mu\mu}\mathrm{d}y_{\mu\mu}}},

(10)

where
N_{\mathrm{evt}}
is the total number of minimum-bias events for a given centrality interval and the values of the mean nuclear overlap function
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
are provided in Section
3
.

The non-prompt fraction,
f^{\mathrm{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}_{\mathrm{NP}}
, is defined as the ratio of corrected yield of non-prompt (NP)
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons to the sum of corrected prompt (P) and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yields,

f^{\mathrm{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}_{\mathrm{NP}}=\frac{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{NP,corr}}}{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{NP,corr}}+N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{P,corr}}}.

(11)

The isolated fraction,
f^{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}_{\mathrm{iso}}
, is defined as the ratio of corrected yield of isolated (iso)
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons to the corrected inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yields (inc),

f^{\mathrm{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}_{\mathrm{iso}}=\frac{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{iso,corr}}}{N_{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}^{\mathrm{inc,corr}}}.

(12)

To obtain statistical uncertainties of
f^{\mathrm{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}}_{\mathrm{NP}}
and
f^{\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}}_{\mathrm{iso}}
, the binomial error propagation is used.

5
Systematic uncertainties

The following sources of systematic uncertainties are considered in this measurement:

\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
kinematic acceptance uncertainty, muon reconstruction efficiency uncertainty, muon trigger efficiency uncertainty, fit model uncertainty, jet-related uncertainties, UE/MPI subtraction uncertainty, uncertainty in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
luminosity, and
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
uncertainty. Systematic uncertainties in the measured distributions can be categorised into two classes: bin-wise correlated uncertainties and uncertainties that affect the overall normalisation of distributions. Uncertainties due to the determination of
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
luminosity belong to the second class, all other uncertainties belong to the first. The systematic uncertainties are propagated using knowledge of the correlations among sources, leading to an appropriate reduction in systematic uncertainties in ratios.

5.1
Acceptance uncertainty

The uncertainty in acceptance has three components. The first component evaluates the impact of spin alignment on the acceptance. To measure that, six alternative scenarios are evaluated corresponding to extreme cases of spin-alignment as described in Ref.
[
50
]
. The uncertainty is calculated as the maximum deviation from the assumption of unpolarised production. Since the polarisation of charmonia in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions is measured to be small
[
53
,
52
,
51
]
, its modification due to the nuclear environment is neglected, and the spin-alignment uncertainty is assumed to cancel out in ratios involving
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
Pb
+
Pb
yields. This uncertainty is presented on the cross-sections and yields separately from other uncertainties. The second source is the acceptance interpolation by Equation
2
, which may introduce an artificial slope to the background when the signal-to-background ratio is low. To address this potential bias, an alternative set of weights is derived without the interpolation. The last component is connected to the FSR as described in Section
4.1
. The FSR correction is derived in the full rapidity bin
|y|<2
, and its systematic uncertainty arises from the residual rapidity dependence, which is studied. The acceptance uncertainties are fully cancelled in all ratios, they manifest themselves only in the per-event yields in
Pb
+
Pb
and cross-section in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions. The dominant systematic uncertainty corresponds to the FSR uncertainty.

5.2
Muon reconstruction efficiency uncertainty

The systematic uncertainty associated with the muon reconstruction efficiency in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions accounts for possible biases in the tag-and-probe method used to evaluate it. The uncertainty is lower than 1%, and more details of its estimation are given in Ref.
[
54
]
.

Several sources of uncertainty are considered in evaluating the total muon reconstruction efficiency uncertainty in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions. The muon reconstruction efficiency uncertainty is evaluated following Ref.
[
54
]
by changing the tag-muon selection criteria and modifying the line shapes in the extraction fit procedure. The statistical uncertainties of the scale factors are also included as a source of the systematic uncertainty. Due to the small probe yield in the
Pb
+
Pb
data, the statistical uncertainties of the scale factors dominate the uncertainty in the muon reconstruction efficiency correction.
The final systematic uncertainty is evaluated as a quadrature sum of individual sources and propagated through the data-to-MC scale factors.

5.3
Muon trigger efficiency uncertainty

The systematic uncertainty in the trigger efficiency correction is estimated in a similar manner to the uncertainty in the muon reconstruction efficiency. The fit model and tag-muon selection criteria are modified in the tag-and-probe method. For the muon trigger efficiency in the
Pb
+
Pb
, an additional uncertainty associated with the centrality-dependent correction is added to the total systematic uncertainty. Statistical uncertainties in the muon trigger efficiency scale factors are also propagated to the final result. All sources of muon trigger systematic uncertainties are combined in quadrature and propagated through the data-to-MC scale factors.

5.4
Fit model uncertainty

The uncertainty associated with the fit model is obtained by relaxing its parameter constraints and introducing alternative PDFs. In each modification of the fit model, all measured quantities are recalculated and compared with the nominal fit. In total, ten variations of the fit model are considered. The weighted sum of the CB distribution and the Gaussian distribution is changed to the weighted sum of two Gaussian distributions, and the parameters of the CB distribution, which were originally fixed, are left free. The scaling factor connecting the width of Gaussian distribution with the width parameter of CB distribution is varied. For the background
m_{\mu\mu}
PDF, the single exponential distribution is replaced by the first-order Chebyshev polynomial. For the time-resolution PDF, the double Gaussian distribution is replaced with a single Gaussian distribution, and the width parameter is varied. The dominant source of uncertainty is the variation in the signal mass shape. The root-mean-square of all variations is then assigned as the fit model systematic uncertainty.

5.5
Jet correction uncertainty

The jet-related uncertainties have three sources: JES, JER, and the shape of the jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum.
The JES uncertainty has three components. The first component is a centrality-independent baseline determined from calorimetric studies
[
61
]
plus relative energy-scale differences between heavy-ion and 13
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
jet reconstruction
[
62
]
. The second component accounts for the quark/gluon composition of parton showers
[
59
]
. The third, centrality-dependent component, accounts for a different calorimetric response to jets with a modified parton shower due to quenching in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions
[
62
,
59
]
.
The JER uncertainty is derived from dijet energy-balance studies in 13
TeV

\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data
[
61
]
and includes additional contributions to account for the different heavy-ion jet reconstruction procedure. The JES and JER uncertainties are propagated by modifying the jet
p_{\text{T}}
threshold correction factors. Since the jet
p_{\text{T}}
threshold correction is sensitive to the shape of the jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum, the reconstructed jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum generated in MC is reweighted to match the jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum measured in data as described in Section
4.2
. To assess the sensitivity of the result to this difference, the evaluation of the jet
p_{\text{T}}
threshold correction is repeated with the unreweighted jet
p_{\text{T}}
spectrum. The dominant jet-related uncertainty is JER.

5.6
Background subtraction uncertainty

The uncertainty in the jet background subtraction, described in Section
4.3
, is evaluated by a variation of the background jet rate within the overall statistical uncertainty. For
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions, this uncertainty is also studied and found to be negligible.

5.7
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
luminosity and
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
uncertainties

The uncertainty in
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
arises from geometric modelling uncertainties (e.g. nucleon–nucleon inelastic cross section, Woods–Saxon parameterisation of the nucleon distribution) and the uncertainty in the fraction of selected inelastic Pb+Pb collisions.
The values of these uncertainties are presented in Table
1
. The integrated luminosity of
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
data is calibrated using data from dedicated beam separation scans and is determined using procedures described in Ref.
[
69
]
. The relative systematic uncertainty is 1.0%.

Table

summarises the magnitude of bin-wise correlated systematic uncertainties in measured prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
yields in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and
Pb
+
Pb
collisions. The corresponding uncertainties for non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production are of similar magnitude.

summarises the magnitudes of bin-wise systematic uncertainties for the inclusive prompt and non-prompt

, whereas Figure

summarises the magnitudes of bin-wise systematic uncertainties for the non-isolated prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

.

6
Results

To study the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production mechanism, yields normalised by
N_{\mathrm{evt}}
and
\langle T_{\text{AA}}\rangle
, measured in four centrality intervals of Pb+Pb collisions, and cross sections measured in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions are shown in Figure
5
.
Yields and cross sections evaluated as a function of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
are reported for prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and for three classes characterising the correlation of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
production with the jet production: non-isolated, isolated, and inclusive. A similar power-law-like decrease in measured distributions can be seen for inclusive and isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons, while an increasing trend in the distributions with increasing
p_{\text{T}}
in the region of
p_{\text{T}}<20

GeV
is seen for non-isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons. This increasing trend may be interpreted as a consequence of the increasing phase-space accessibility of the selected
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
+jet topology, which is restricted by the requirement of an accompanying jet with
p_{\text{T}}>20

GeV
. This requirement becomes less restrictive with increasing
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
.

Yields and cross-sections reported in Figure
5
are used to evaluate the fraction of isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
(isolated fraction) defined in Section
4.4
, which is reported in Figure
6
. Overall, the isolated fraction starts at a value of 1 for
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with
p_{\text{T}}\approx 10

GeV
and decreases with increasing
p_{\text{T}}
, reaching a value between 0.6 and 0.8 at
p_{\text{T}}\approx 50

GeV
.
The increasing trend in distributions in Figure
5
attributed to the opening of the phase-space accessibility suggests that full isolation seen for
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
with
p_{\text{T}}<20

GeV
is largely connected with the requirement on minimum jet
p_{\text{T}}
of 20
GeV
.

The upper panels of Figure
6
compare the isolated fraction between prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
showing that it is systematically 10–20% larger for prompt compared to non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons. No differences in the magnitude of the isolated fraction are found between Pb+Pb and
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions and among different centrality intervals of Pb+Pb as shown in the middle panels and lower panels of Figure
6
, respectively. The middle panels also show a comparison with the LO QCD prediction from
Pythia
8. This generator-level study is performed using the same kinematic selection criteria as those applied to the data. It is shown that
Pythia
8 overestimates the isolated fraction for the prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, but provides a good prediction of the isolated fraction for the non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
.

The non-prompt fraction for inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
measured in four centrality intervals of Pb+Pb collisions is shown in the left panel of Figure
7
. The non-prompt fraction starts at a value of
\approx 0.3
at
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
of 10
GeV
, then it increases, reaching a value of approximately 0.6 at
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}\approx 50

GeV
. No significant centrality dependence is observed as previously measured in Ref.
[
11
]
. The non-prompt fraction for inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
measured in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions and 0–80%
Pb
+
Pb
collisions is shown in the right panel of Figure
7
. The non-prompt fraction measured in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions has a slightly different slope from the one measured in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions, as observed before in Ref.
[
11
]
. Left and right panels of Figure
8
show the non-prompt fraction in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
and 0–80% Pb+Pb collisions, respectively, evaluated for non-isolated, isolated, and inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons. While the non-prompt fraction is consistent between isolated and inclusive classes, it is significantly different for the case of non-isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. For
p_{\text{T}}>15

GeV
, the non-prompt fraction of non-isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is consistent with no
p_{\text{T}}
dependence, and it reaches a value of approximately 0.7.
These results provide complementary information to the evaluation of the isolated fraction presented in Figure
6
.

The
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
constructed from yields measured in
Pb
+
Pb
collisions and cross sections in
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
\mathit{{p}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
collisions evaluated as a function of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
, is shown for inclusive prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in Figure
9
. These new results improve the quantification of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
suppression for
p_{\text{T}}>30

GeV
with the
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
values reaching approximately 0.6 and 0.4 at
p_{\text{T}}\approx 50

GeV
for prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, respectively, in the 0–10% centrality interval.

The
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
evaluated as a function of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}

p_{\text{T}}
for non-isolated, isolated and inclusive
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
in the 0–80% centrality interval is shown in Figure
10
, and figures of the
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
for other centrality intervals are presented in the Appendix of this paper. The two panels of Figure
10
compare prompt and non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. In both cases, one can see that the
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
is consistent between the isolated and the inclusive case in the whole
p_{\text{T}}
range. For
p_{\text{T}}>30

GeV
, the central values of
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
of non-isolated prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
appear to be systematically lower than those for isolated prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
. To measure this difference, the
p_{\text{T}}
dependence of
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
is fitted separately for the isolated and non-isolated samples using polynomial functions. The quadratic and higher-order terms are consistent with zero within uncertainties, indicating that linear functions provide a good description of the data in the kinematic range considered. The slopes extracted for the isolated and non-isolated samples differ by 3.3 standard deviations for the 0–80% centrality interval as well as for the individual centrality intervals excluding the most peripheral one. As a cross-check, Stouffer’s test
[
70
,
71
]
is applied directly to the data points, yielding a significance of 3.5 standard deviations.
Such a difference might be interpreted as a larger suppression of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
produced in association with
more developed parton shower or fragmentation system.
In the case of non-prompt
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
, no differences among the three isolation classes of
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
mesons are seen. A difference between non-isolated and isolated
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
is also seen in
R_{{}\mathrm{AA}}
for

GeV
for both prompt and non-prompt cases with a smaller significance than in the prompt high-

in that region tends to grow, which may be connected with a complex interplay between phase-space opening and quenching of jets used to tag the
\mathit{{J\mspace{-2mu}/\mspace{-2mu}\psi}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}
.
